It is not\nstandard ESM, it prevents tree-shaking, and it breaks Node's native TypeScript\nrunner. Use flat top-level exports combined with a self-reexport at the bottom\nof the file:\n\n```ts\n// src/foo/foo.ts\nexport interface Interface { ... }\nexport class Service extends Context.Service<Service, Interface>()(\"@opencode/Foo\") {}\nexport const layer = Layer.effect(Service, ...)\nexport const defaultLayer = layer.pipe(...)\n\nexport * as Foo from \"./foo\"\n```\n\nConsumers import the namespace projection:\n\n```ts\nimport { Foo } from \"@/foo/foo\"\n\nyield * Foo.Service\nFoo.layer\nFoo.defaultLayer\n```\n\nNamespace-private helpers stay as non-exported top-level declarations in the\nsame file — they remain inaccessible to consumers (they are not projected by\n`export * as`) but are usable by the file's own code.\n\n## When the file is an `index.ts`\n\nIf the module is `foo/index.ts` (single-namespace directory), use `\".\"` for\nthe self-reexport source rather than `\"./index\"`:\n\n```ts\n// src/foo/index.ts\nexport const thing = ...\n\nexport * as Foo from \".\"\n```\n\n## Multi-sibling directories\n\nFor directories with several independent modules (e.g. `src/session/`,\n`src/config/`), keep each sibling as its own file with its own self-reexport,\nand do not add a barrel `index.ts`. Consumers import the specific sibling:\n\n```ts\nimport { SessionRetry } from \"@/session/retry\"\nimport { SessionStatus } from \"@/session/status\"\n```\n\nBarrels in multi-sibling directories force every import through the barrel to\nevaluate every sibling, which defeats tree-shaking and slows module load.\n\n# opencode Effect rules\n\nUse these rules when writing or migrating Effect code.\n\nSee `specs/effect/migration.md` for the compact pattern reference and examples.\n\n## Core\n\n- Use `Effect.gen(function* () { ... })` for composition.\n- Use `Effect.fn(\"Domain.method\")` for named/traced effects and `Effect.fnUntraced` for internal helpers.\n- `Effect.fn` / `Effect.fnUntraced` accept pipeable operators as extra arguments, so avoid unnecessary outer `.pipe()` wrappers.\n- Use `Effect.callback` for callback-based APIs.\n- Use `Effect.void` instead of `Effect.succeed(undefined)` or `Effect.succeed(void 0)`.\n- Prefer `DateTime.nowAsDate` over `new Date(yield* Clock.currentTimeMillis)` when you need a `Date`.\n\n## Module conventions\n\n- In `src/config`, follow the existing self-export pattern at the top of the file (for example `export * as ConfigAgent from \"./agent\"`) when adding a new config module.\n\n## Schemas and errors\n\n- Use `Schema.Class` for multi-field data.\n- Use branded schemas (`Schema.brand`) for single-value types.\n- Use `Schema.TaggedErrorClass` for typed errors.\n- Use `Schema.Defect` instead of `unknown` for defect-like causes.\n- In `Effect.gen` / `Effect.fn`, prefer `yield* new MyError(...)` over `yield* Effect.fail(new MyError(...))` for direct early-failure branches.\n\n## Runtime vs InstanceState\n\n- Use `makeRuntime` (from `src/effect/run-service.ts`) for all services. It returns `{ runPromise, runFork, runCallback }` backed by a shared `memoMap` that deduplicates layers.\n- Use `InstanceState` (from `src/effect/instance-state.ts`) for per-directory or per-project state that needs per-instance cleanup. It uses `ScopedCache` keyed by directory — each open project gets its own state, automatically cleaned up on disposal.\n- If two open directories should not share one copy of the service, it needs `InstanceState`.\n- Do the work directly in the `InstanceState.make` closure — `ScopedCache` handles run-once semantics. Don't add fibers, `ensure()` callbacks, or `started` flags on top.\n- Use `Effect.addFinalizer` or `Effect.acquireRelease` inside the `InstanceState.make` closure for cleanup (subscriptions, process teardown, etc.).\n- Use `Effect.forkScoped` inside the closure for background stream consumers — the fiber is interrupted when the instance is disposed.\n- To make a service's `init()` non-blocking, fork `InstanceState.get(state)` at the `init()` call site (e.g. `Effect.forkIn(scope)`), not by forking work inside the `InstanceState.make` closure. Forking inside the closure leaves state incomplete for other methods that read it.\n- `src/project/bootstrap.ts` already wraps every service `init()` in `Effect.forkDetach`, so `init()` is fire-and-forget in production. Keep `init()` methods synchronous internally; the caller controls concurrency.\n\n## Effect v4 beta API\n\n- `Effect.fork` and `Effect.forkDaemon` do not exist. Use `Effect.forkIn(scope)` to fork a fiber into a specific scope.\n\n## Preferred Effect services\n\n- In effectified services, prefer yielding existing Effect services over dropping down to ad hoc platform APIs.\n- Prefer `FileSystem.FileSystem` instead of raw `fs/promises` for effectful file I/O.\n- Prefer `ChildProcessSpawner.ChildProcessSpawner` with `ChildProcess.make(...)` instead of custom process wrappers.\n- Prefer `HttpClient.HttpClient` instead of raw `fetch`.\n- Prefer `Path.Path`, `Config`, `Clock`, and `DateTime` when those concerns are already inside Effect code.\n- For background loops or scheduled tasks, use `Effect.repeat` or `Effect.schedule` with `Effect.forkScoped` in the layer definition.\n\n## Effect.cached for deduplication\n\nUse `Effect.cached` when multiple concurrent callers should share a single in-flight computation rather than storing `Fiber | undefined` or `Promise | undefined` manually. Do not use slashes or type prefixes such as `feat/` or `fix/`.\n\nExamples: `session-recovery`, `fix-scroll-state`, `regenerate-sdk`.\n\n## Commits and PR Titles\n\nUse conventional commit-style messages and PR titles: `type(scope): summary`.\n\nValid types are `feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `chore`, `refactor`, and `test`. Inline the logic at the call site unless the helper is reused, hides a genuinely complex boundary, or has a clear independent name that improves the caller.\n- Avoid `try`/`catch` where possible\n- Avoid using the `any` type\n- Use Bun APIs when possible, like `Bun.file()`\n- Rely on type inference when possible; avoid explicit type annotations or interfaces unless necessary for exports or clarity\n- Prefer functional array methods (flatMap, filter, map) over for loops; use type guards on filter to maintain type inference downstream\n- In `src/config`, follow the existing self-export pattern at the top of the file (for example `export * as ConfigAgent from \"./agent\"`) when adding a new config module.\n- In Effect generators, bind services to named variables before calling methods. Use dot notation to preserve context.\n\n```ts\n// Good\nobj.a\nobj.b\n\n// Bad\nconst { a, b } = obj\n```\n\n### Imports\n\n- Never alias imports. Do not use `import { foo as bar } from \"...\"` or renamed imports like `resolve as pathResolve`.\n- Never use star imports. Do not use `import * as Foo from \"...\"` or `import type * as Foo from \"...\"`.\n- If a namespace-style value is needed, import the module's own exported namespace by name, for example `import { Project } from \"@opencode-ai/core/project\"`, then reference `Project.ID`.\n- Prefer dynamic imports for heavy modules that are only needed in selected code paths, especially in startup-sensitive entrypoints. Destructure dynamic import bindings near the top of the narrowest scope that needs them so they read like normal imports. Avoid inline chains such as `await import(\"./module\").then((mod) => mod.value())` or `(await import(\"./module\")).value()`. Keep branch-specific imports inside the branch that needs them to preserve lazy loading.\n\n### Variables\n\nPrefer `const` over `let`. Use ternaries or early returns instead of reassignment.\n\n```ts\n// Good\nconst foo = condition ? 1 : 2\n\n// Bad\nlet foo\nif (condition) foo = 1\nelse foo = 2\n```\n\n### Control Flow\n\nAvoid `else` statements. Prefer early returns.\n\n```ts\n// Good\nfunction foo() {\n  if (condition) return 1\n  return 2\n}\n\n// Bad\nfunction foo() {\n  if (condition) return 1\n  else return 2\n}\n```\n\n### Complex Logic\n\nWhen a function has several validation branches or supporting details, make the main function read as the happy path and move supporting details into small helpers below it.\n\n```ts\n// Good\nexport function loadThing(input: unknown) {\n  const config = requireConfig(input)\n  const metadata = readMetadata(input)\n  return createThing({ config, metadata })\n}\n\nfunction requireConfig(input: unknown) {\n  ...\n}\n```\n\n- Keep helpers close to the code they support, below the main export when that improves readability.\n- Do not over-abstract simple expressions into many single-use helpers; extract only when it names a real concept like `requireConfig` or `readMetadata`.\n- Do not return `Effect` from helpers unless they actually perform effectful work. Synchronous parsing, validation, and option building should stay synchronous.\n- Prefer Effect schema helpers such as `Schema.UnknownFromJsonString` and `Schema.decodeUnknownOption` over manual `JSON.parse` wrapped in `Effect.try` when parsing untrusted JSON strings.\n- Add comments for non-obvious constraints and surprising behavior, not for obvious assignments or control flow.\n\n### Schema Definitions (Drizzle)\n\nUse snake_case for field names so column names don't need to be redefined as strings.\n\n```ts\n// Good\nconst table = sqliteTable(\"session\", {\n  id: text().primaryKey(),\n  project_id: text().notNull(),\n  created_at: integer().notNull(),\n})\n\n// Bad\nconst table = sqliteTable(\"session\", {\n  id: text(\"id\").primaryKey(),\n  projectID: text(\"project_id\").notNull(),\n  createdAt: integer(\"created_at\").notNull(),\n})\n```\n\n## Testing\n\n- Avoid mocks as much as possible, you shouldn't be using globalThis.\\* at all unless it's the only option.\n- Test actual implementation, do not duplicate logic into tests\n- Tests cannot run from repo root (guard: `do-not-run-tests-from-root`); run from package dirs like `packages/opencode`.\n\n## Type Checking\n\n- Always run `bun typecheck` from package directories (e.g., `packages/opencode`), never `tsc` directly.\n\n## V2 Session Core\n\n- Keep durable prompt admission separate from model execution. `SessionV2.prompt(...)` admits one durable `session_input` row before scheduling advisory `SessionExecution.wake(sessionID)` unless `resume: false` requests admit-only behavior. Python 3.8+.\\n\\n## Examples\\n\\nGiven `example.csv`:\\n\\n```csv\\nname,age,score\\nalice,30,9.5\\nbob,25,8.25\\nalice,22,7.0\\n```\\n\\nTable output:\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum example.csv\\ncolumn  type     count  min     max     mean    median  distinct\\n------  -------  -----  -----   -----   -----   ------  --------\\nname    text     3                                            2\\nage     numeric  3      22.000  30.000  25.667  25.000\\nscore   numeric  3      7.000   9.500   8.250   8.250\\n```\\n\\nRestrict to one column:\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum --column age example.csv\\ncolumn  type     count  min     max     mean    median  distinct\\n------  -------  -----  -----   -----   -----   ------  --------\\nage     numeric  3      22.000  30.000  25.667  25.000\\n```\\n\\nRepeat `--column` for several columns (output follows the requested order):\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum --column score --column age example.csv\\n```\\n\\nMachine-readable JSON:\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum --json example.csv\\n{\\n  \\\"columns\\\": [\\n    {\\n      \\\"name\\\": \\\"name\\\",\\n      \\\"type\\\": \\\"text\\\",\\n      \\\"count\\\": 3,\\n      \\\"distinct\\\": 2\\n    },\\n    {\\n      \\\"name\\\": \\\"age\\\",\\n      \\\"type\\\": \\\"numeric\\\",\\n      \\\"count\\\": 3,\\n      \\\"min\\\": 22.0,\\n      \\\"max\\\": 30.0,\\n      \\\"mean\\\": 25.667,\\n      \\\"median\\\": 25.0\\n    }\\n  ]\\n}\\n```\\n\\nCombine with `--column`:\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum --json --column age example.csv\\n```\\n\\nRead from stdin (no file argument, or explicit `-`):\\n\\n```bash\\n$ cat example.csv | python -m tsum\\n$ cat example.csv | python -m tsum -\\n$ python -m tsum a.csv - < extra.csv\\n```\\n\\nMultiple files are merged. All files must share the same header (same names,\\nsame order):\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum jan.csv feb.csv\\n```\\n\\n## Errors (non-zero exit)\\n\\nAll errors print `tsum: error: ...` to stderr and exit with status 1:\\n\\n| Case | Example message |\\n|------|-----------------|\\n| Missing file | `tsum: error: file not found: nope.csv` |\\n| Empty file / header only / empty stdin | `tsum: error: empty.csv: empty file (no data rows)` |\\n| Ragged row | `tsum: error: data.csv: line 3: expected 2 fields but got 1 (ragged row)` |\\n| Header mismatch | `tsum: error: feb.csv: header mismatch: expected ['a'] but got ['b']` |\\n| Unknown `--column` | `tsum: error: column not found: 'height'` |\\n\\n```bash\\n$ python -m tsum missing.csv; echo $?\\ntsum: error: file not found: missing.csv\\n1\\n```\\n\\n## Tests\\n\\n```bash\\npytest tsum/test_tsum.py -v\\n# or from inside tsum/:\\npytest -v\\n```\\n\",\"filePath\":\"/Users/mirmahathirmohammad/Documents/ocmini/packages/opencode/tsum/README.md\"}"
